MEETING: Sine Die Meeting | DATE: 2015-01-05 | CLIP_ID: 462 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 462

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 5, 2015
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in “Sine Die” Session on this date at 2:30
p.m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  T hose p resent w ere: M AYOR:  Richard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS: Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present were Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Following the pledge of allegiance, Mayor Kaysen welcomed those in attendance and stated
the purpose of the session was to ceremonially recognize outgoing Council members (whose terms
of office were expiring) for their years of service on City Council.  He expressed appreciation to
outgoing Council members Ms. Broyles and Mr. Allen for their time, effort and dedication to the
community.
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, read a ceremonial resolution in its
entirety honoring  outgoing  Council member Ge orgia Adams B royles, entitled as follows: “A
Ceremonial Resolution in Recognition of the Honorable Georgia Adams Broyles for her Dedication
and Outstanding Contributions to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and its Residents.”  Mayor Kaysen
presented Ms. Broyles with a framed version of the resolution, noting her 10 years of service as a
member of City Council and thanking her for her service to the community.  Ms. Broyles commented
on her years of service as a City Council member, and expressed appreciation to the voters, past and
present members of the g overning body who had se rved with her, City staff, a nd members of  her
family for their support. (#5643)
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, Ms. Intlekofer read a ceremonial resolution in its entirety honoring
outgoing Council member Sean Allen, entitled as follows:  “A Ceremonial Resolution in Recognition
of the Honor able Sean D. Allen for  his Dedica tion and Outstanding  Contributions to the  City of
Cheyenne, Wyoming, and its Residents.”  Mayor Kaysen presented Mr. Allen with a framed version
of the resolution, noting his 4 years of service as a member of City Council and thanking him for his
service to the community.  Mr. Allen commented on his years of service, and expressed appreciation
for the oppor tunity to serve the  community  as a City Council member, and to membe rs of the
governing body who had served with him, City staff, and members of his family for their support.
(#5644)
Mayor Kaysen announced the First Regular Meeting of the governing body, involving newly
elected and continuing members, would convene in City Council Chambers immediately following
the closure of the “Sine Die” session.
There being no further business previously noted for the “Sine Die” Session, Mayor Kaysen
adjourned the meeting at 2:48 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
PUBLISH: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 January 9, 2015

---

MEETING: First Regular Meeting of the Governing Body | DATE: 2015-01-05 | CLIP_ID: 463 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 463

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 
January 5, 2015
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne assembled for its First Regular Meeting for
2015 on this date at 2:49 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Purpose of the First Regular Meeting was to
recognize and administer oaths of office to those individuals who were elected, or re-elected, at the
most r ecent general e lection to s erve a s Ci ty Cou ncil m embers, a s w ell a s p rovide fo r certain
administrative functions.
Mayor Kaysen welcomed those in attendance and introduced The Honorable Thomas L. Lee,
Circuit Court Judge, 1st Judicial District, who was present to administer oaths of office to officials.
Judge Lee expressed appreciation for community services provided by local elected officials and
administered the oath of elected office to the following newly elected, or re-elected, City Council
members: Scott D. Roybal, Ward I; Dr. Mark D. Rinne, Ward II; and Richard Johnson, Ward III.
Mayor Kaysen verified that the recently elected Council members had received their oath of
office as required by law and officially called the First Regular Meeting of the governing body to
order at 2:55 p.m.  Those present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr.
Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms.
Williams.  Also present we re Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Da n White, City Attorney.  The
pledge of allegiance was recited.
Election of City Council President for 2015.  Mayor Kaysen announced, pursuant to City
Code, the mayor conducts the election for Council President but does not cast a  vote.  Mr. Cook
moved to nominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City Council President for 2015, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Dr. Rinne accepted the nomination.  Mr. Roybal moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr.
Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of
Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  Motion to nominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City Council President
for 2015 carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor
Kaysen who did not vote.
Election of City Council Vice-President for 2015.  Mayor Kaysen announced, pursuant to
City Code, the mayor conducts the election for Council Vice-President but does not cast a vote.  Dr.
Rinne moved to nominate and elect Mr. Shanor as City Council Vice-President for 2015, seconded
by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Shanor accepted the nomination.  Dr. Rinne moved that nominations cease,
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with the
exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  Motion to nominate and elect Mr. Shanor as City
Council Vice-President for 2015 carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with
the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
Approval of Appointment of City  officials (pursuant to Title 2 of the Code of  the City of
Cheyenne) as follows:  Matthew J. Ashby, Planning Services Director; Paul S. Galeotos, Municipal
Court Judge; Randy K. Hays, Assistant City Treasurer (Board of Public Utilities); Lois A. Huff, City
Treasurer; Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk/Assistant City Treasurer; Ronnald J. Jeffrey, Juvenile Court
Judge; Brian N. Kozak, Chief of the Cheyenne Police Department; James A. Martin, Chief of the
Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department; Mark T. Moran, Municipal Court Judge; Vicki Nemecek,
Public Works Director; Richard L. Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, and Daniel E. White, City
Attorney.  Mr. Brown  moved to appr ove the appointments “en masse”, seconded by Mr. Luna.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with the exception of May or
Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointments).  Following announcement of the vote, Judge Lee
administered the oath of appointed office to those City officials present.  Mayor Kaysen noted that
Mr. Hays was unable to attend the meeting and his oath of office would be administered by the City
Clerk at a later date.
Mayor Kaysen announced light refreshments would be served in the Council annex adjoining
Council Chambers immediately following the meeting.
There being no further business previously noted for the First Regular Meeting, the meeting
was officially adjourned at 3:08 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 January 9, 2015

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 12, 2015
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor,
Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also  present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed  from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal, agenda item #24(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Brown
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 22, 2014.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Sine Die Session of the Gove rning Body on January 5, 2015.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from First Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 5, 2015.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Lois Huff provided
information on vouchers payable to:   Ameri-Tech HVAC Services (equipment replacements paid
from special distribution funds for one time expenditures); AMPO (professional membership dues
to Association of Metropolitan Planning Organizations),  and Lock Shop of Cheyenne, Inc. (spare
keys for newer trucks).  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present
in C ouncil Cha mbers.  F ollowing a nnouncement o f t he vo te, M r. B rown re turned to  Cou ncil
Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for input and comments on a transfer of ownership
for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Mason Properties, Inc.
(d/b/a to be determined), 2801 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by KZ
Corporation d/b/a Daisy Duke’s Bar, 2801 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Upon inquiry,
John Mason, applicant, briefly reviewed adjacent property he owns and improvements he has made;
advised he had previously owned the building and held the liquor license years ago, and stated his
intent for use of the license will be to tear down the existing building and rebuild for use as a small
retail plaza, which will include a pac kage l iquor store.  The re being no f urther comments and
purpose of  the hea ring having  been me t, May or Ka ysen closed the  h earing and re ferred the
application to the Finance Committee.
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion of the NW1/4 of

Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#4054)
AN ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning  Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2,

Agreement, with Cheyenne Rail Hub, LLC, for portions of the Northwest 1/4 of Section 27 and the

RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Thomas
Heights, a replat of Lots 1, 2 and 3, Smithson View, Tracts 26 and 27, and a portion of Tracts 1 and
7, Dell Range Addition, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between Ridge
Road and C ollege D rive, south of Summit Drive).  Ga il Sundell questi oned timing of
implementation of the Level 3 screening; cash-in-lieu of open space agreement; possibility of an exit
from Carla Drive into Thomas Heights, and sidewalks from Thomas Heights south into the City.
Brad Emmons, AVI, agent for a pplicant, Guardian Companies, advised the de veloper would be
building a  6 f oot pr ivacy fe nce a long the  no rth li ne, a nd pr ovided in formation on L evel 3
buffering/landscaping require ments and the c ash-in-lieu of  open spac e agreement, noting total
amounts of useable open space have not yet been calculated and would be reviewed during the
design phase.   Mr. Emmons provided history on why a road from Carla Drive was not planned based
upon past development action, and stated sidewalks would be provided as required, indicating any
sidewalks along College Drive would be a WYDOT decision as it is a state highway under the state’s
jurisdiction, noting that WYDOT staff would be involved in reviewing the plans.  Kay McAdams
commented on developer benefits; requested the name Sailor Street be removed from the plat;
expressed that she wants to be able to do what she wants with her property in the future relative to
street names and use of her property, and indicated she would like assurance from the developer that
this is going to be a quality housing development.  Mr. Emmons stated one of the owners involved
in the develo pment project requested Sailor Street be the name of one of the streets within the
subdivision, and that there are no plans to change this.  He submitted a copy of the letter from the
respective owners requesting the stree t name ( Note: Copy on file with the City  Clerk’s Of fice).
James Perrott Macneil stated he was exercising power of attorney and forbid the City from using any
land he owns.  During Mr. Macneil’s comments, Mr. Brown raised a point of order that comments
were off subject from the agenda topic with Mayor Kaysen concurring with the point of order.  Karen
Larson expressed her concerns with current traffic speed and control measures on College Drive, and
street da rkness, due  to lack of lig hting, on Thoma s Road at Colleg e Dr ive and Ridg e Road
intersections; questioned whether some type of signaling mechanism for fire trucks exiting from the
station on College Drive could be installed, and commented in support of open public space within
the development.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt contingent upon a cash-in-lieu agreement involving
parks fees, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Emmons stated the City has a franchise
agreement with Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power (CLFP) and when the design is done they will work
with CLFP and other utility providers with regard to roads, lighting, services and placement of water
and sewer.  Mr. Emmons also provided information on planned detention ponds and potential use
of some as open space areas.  Chief Jim Martin, Fire and Rescue Department, advised the fire station
on College Drive is outside City limits.  He stated once the Thomas Heights development area is
annexed it becomes the City’s responsibility for Fire and Rescue response but they do have a mutual
aid agreement with the county (re. fire stations) if a re quest by the City for assistance is needed.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5647 RES/#6278 CON)

Professional Services Agreement between the Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization
and A.V.I., p.c., for the High Plains Road 35% Design Plan (MPO Planning funds).  James Perrot
Macneil commented that he owned  all of the land ( i.e. involving the f uture road).  Mr. B rown
reported there was no recommendation from Finance Committee on the item due to lack of a second
on the motion.  Mr. Brown moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $85,300.00, seconded by
Mr. Roy bal.  Mr. Shanor move d to amend Exhibi t A (Scope of  Work), Task D.2, Steer ing
Committee Meetings, by adding a condition that Councilman Luna be a member of the Steering
Committee, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Luna confirmed he is interested in the
project and would like to serve on the committee.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting "yes" -- all
members of the governing body.  Discussion topics included:  2004 South Cheyenne Streets Master
Plan; connectivity between I-25 (near Swan Ranch interchange) and South Greeley Highway; past
County replatting of Spee dway Road; eff icient use of taxpayers dollars, and planning  for f uture
growth in the ar ea.  U pon inquiry, Sreyoshi Chakraborty, Metropolitan Planning O rganization
(MPO), advised the intent of the corridor plan is to begin design of a future road to connect with the
High Plains interchange and that the plan will provide for a proposed route and general alignment
to businesses and developments in the area.  Tom Mason, MPO Director, provided information on
the proposed r oadway location g enerally (betwe en I -25 and South Gre eley Hig hway) and
approximate distance involved (four miles), stating most of the land currently is open pra irie;
reviewed and discussed the 2004 South Cheyenne Streets Master Plan and the need to take the plan
to a 35% level for the High Plains Road portion, and described plan elements that would be studied
and included in the design plan, such as road width, drainage and engineering details.  He explained
discussions with landowners relative to future acquisition of any needed rights-of-way would also
be conducted.  Ma yor Kaysen noted a map would be forthcoming once the design is created to
determine where the alignment will go.   Main motion, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams voting "no".
(#6276)

Change Order No. 1 to acc epted Bid #R-16-14 from Peterbilt of Wy oming, Cheyenne,
Wyoming, for cab and chassis units with automated refuse collection packer body with dual arm side
loaders for the City of Cheyenne (Solid Waste Equipment Lease) -- referred to Finance Committee.
Mr. Brown advised the item was discussed under other items for the agenda at Finance Committee
and that provisions of Title 2 of City Code had been met.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director,
advised the change order will bring the equipment lease agreement int o compliance with state
statute, advising that recent restructuring of the lease now requires the financial institution own and
title the equipment and that the trade-in process must be a separate financial exchange.  She stated
the change order involves three trade-ins in which the C ity will retain one of the trucks, due to a
recent truck f ire, and that the trucks ha ve been received and placed into service.  Ms. Nemec ek
explained approval of the change order will allow for payment to the vendor.  Mr. Brown moved to
approve in the amount of $68,500.00, seconded byMr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek advised
the truck that was damaged due to the fire will be declared as surplus.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes"
-- all members of the governing body.

for one new 2014 - 2015 utility farm tractor for the City of Cheyenne, Landfill Division (Solid Waste
Equipment Lease)  -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Authorization to purchase one John Deere 772G Motor Grader with 6wd, new 2015 model
under Wyoming Department of Transportation Bid #15-038MD, for the Street and Alley Division
(2011-2014 1%  Sales Tax ).  Motion (per  Consent Ag enda) car ried (a mount not to ex ceed
$273,156.00).   
[CA] Consideration o f B id # M-8-15 fo r a  c ontract be tween th e City  of  Che yenne, Pa rks &
Recreation Department, and Construction Specialists, Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Airport Golf
Shop Grinding Room Addition Project (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax, Golf Capital Equipment Fund) --
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-18-15 for furnishing eight new thermal imaging cameras for the City
of Cheyenne, Fire and Rescue D epartment (2011 - 2014 1%  Sales Tax) -- re ferred to F inance
Committee. 
[CA] Consideration o f B id # M-11-15 f or a  c ontract b etween th e Ci ty o f Ch eyenne, Police
Department, and Beka Corporation, Rapid City, South Dakota, for f iber optics for the Cheyenne
Public Safety Center Projec t (1% Specif ic Purpose Sales &  Use Ta x) --  ref erred to Fina nce
Committee. 
Re-Appointments of Russell Pollard to the Police Civil Service Commission; Ann Esquibel
Redman to  the   Pe rsonnel Co mmission, a nd Ric k Amm on a nd Wes  Pe terson to t he Hi storic
Preservation Board.  Mr. L una moved to appr ove, seconded by Ms. Wi lliams.  Moti on carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not
vote (Mayoral appointees). 
Appointments of Kathryn O. Fay and Christina Bird to the Historic Preservation Board, and
George Parks to the Police Civil Service Commission.  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by
Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception
of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointees). 
[CA] Consideration of purchase from Improve Group, Inc., to provide and install high density
mobile shelving in the Property and Evidence Division for the new Cheyenne Public Safety Center
(1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.  
 
Announcement was made  of the Re- Appointment of Ted Ge rtsch to t he Housing a nd
Community Development Advisory Council, to be confirmed January 26, 2015. 
Announcement was made of the Appointments of Nicholas A. Zuhner to the International
Fire Code Board of Appeals, William Sean Casey and Gabriel Herrera to the Building Code Board
of Appeals; Caroline Alston, Angel Antunez, Elizabeth Babbitt, Molly Black, Alex Cole, Shayna
Engdahl, Alexandra Farkas, Lexi Ferrari, Liam Guille, Rachel Moseley, Yvette Moyte, Emily Piel,
Aly Rowe, Jason Weber, Darnell Wheeler Jr., and Katelyn Williams to the Mayor’s Youth Council;
to be confirmed January 26, 2015. 
Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King, J r. Holiday on Monday,
January 19, 2015, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the
Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance
Committee will meet Tuesday, January 20, 2015 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will
meet Wednesday, January 21, 2015 at Noon. 
Under other business, Deb Kellam, Martha Silver and Ben Silver expressed concerns with
bicycle lane striping planne d for Car ey and Pioneer  Avenues in conjunction with the On-Stree t
Bicycle Striping and Signage Project.  Comments included the following topics:  Safety of bicyclists;
street obstacles affecting bicyclists; re-evaluating the contract that has been approved for striping and
signage se rvices; c omponents of  a  his toric ne ighborhood; hi gh v olume tr affic on the str eets,
including use by emergency responders; grant funding for the pro ject and whether approval was
required by the Historic Preservation Board, and the difference between bicycle lane striping vs.
signage markings.  Mayor Kaysen requested Mr. Wiggins review grant requirements regarding the
inquiry involving the Historic Preservation Board and provide information after his review.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. Wiggins referenced a m ap of streets involved in the bike lane striping and signage
project, explained the different markings (in color on the map) that will be placed on the street and
what they mean, and vehicle parking availability.  He stated Carey and Pioneer Avenues are each
one-way couplets and were selected for the project based on their high value as a connecter route and
input from bicycle users who want better connectivity between downtown and north portion of the
City.  He explained the project will provide for guidance on sharing the roadway for bicyclists and
vehicle drivers; described what will be plac ed on the stre ets for striping  lanes vs. sig nage, and
reviewed the planning process for the project that began in 2010 which focused on ways to improve
the greenway and bicycle lanes to provide a complete loop around the City.  Mr. Wiggins provided
information on review of the project for compliance with bicycle codes and laws, and by WYDOT
and the State Historic Preservation Office; discussed options for providing public education on the
street markings, and commented that video signa l detection at the Per shing intersection will be
implemented.  In response to query on status of the current contract with the company that will be
providing striping and signage services, and whether the contract could be cancelled, Mayor Kaysen
advised the legal department would need to be involved as there are legal considerations to review.
Mr. Shanor moved that a report, in the form of an agenda item, be provided at the January 26, 2015
City Council meeting by the City Attorney and Mr. Wiggins, and any other interested parties, on the
following issues: (1) Gra nt funding requirements relative to the Historic Pre servation Board; (2)
whether a shared lane marking depicted in r ed or orange color and/or the blue  colored bike lane
(colored areas as shown on the previously referenced map) relate to or meet provisions of City Code
Section 10.80.080 which discusses parking on a bike way, and particularly subsection "G", which
states where parking is permitted and, if not, what action needs to be taken, and (3) a memorandum
from the City  attorney, which may  be issued as c onfidential to Council members, on the le gal
implications and processes that would be re quired if there could be an amendment to a c ontract
already agreed to or, if desired, how to end a contract.  The motion was seconded by Mr. Luna.  Ms.
Kellam questioned whether a contract already signed can be amended or negotiated to just have a
stencil marking to focus attention on bicycle use in the area instead of a solid bike lane marking.
Upon inquiry regarding whether street markings are considered to be historic, Nathan Beauheim,
Acting City Engineer, advised the federal highway's perspective is that buildings and properties are
historic but roads, signs, asphalt, etc. are not considered to be historic.  Upon inquiry regarding the
funneling of bicyclists in this area (of the Avenues) and how many bicyclists use the streets, Mr.
Wiggins noted the closest bike count area studied was at 8th and Carey Avenues and he would
provide that infor mation to Ci ty Council.  Comm ents followed r egarding public service
announcements to provide community education on the bike lane striping and signage markings, and
whether a motion was needed vs. administrative review procedures.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes" --
all members of the governing body.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 8:27 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 January 23, 2015

Report on the On-Street Bicycle Striping and Signage Project involving Contract #6270  with
Kolbe Striping, Inc.  Barbara Kloth, Jordan Evans and Travis Kolbo spoke in support of the bike lane
striping proposed for Carey and Pioneer Avenues, and in support generally for the overall project.
Comments included the following topics:  Safety for bicyclists and pedestrians; extensive studies and
work that ha d been a chieved on the appr oved proje ct; alternative modes of tra nsportation;
community importance of a bicycle striping/signage program, and amenities affiliated with attracting
and retaining young professionals and adults in the community.  Upon inquiry, Jeff Wiggins, Parks
and Recreation Department, provided information on a past report, including a "gap analysis", that
had been conducted to review different routes, and potential and identified corridors, and provided
information relating to old signage, and streets used by bicyclists that are not necessarily delineated
to motorists and bicyclists for that use.  Mayor Kaysen stated request for the report had been met.

vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  In conjunction with an item on the voucher listing,

Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of approximately 12.75 acres of land situated as a

45 and 46, Dell Range Addition, 4th Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (located on the northeast

hearing.
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion of the NW1/4 of
Section 21, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known as The Bluffs, 10th
Filing Annexation (located between Converse Avenue and Crestridge Drive, north of Foothills Road)
(Postponed from January 26, 2015).  Kelly Hafner, Benchmark Engineering, agent for the applicant
and pr operty ow ner, Un ited We stern Cor poration, e xpressed a ppreciation to  Da n Wh ite, City
Attorney, for providing feedback to him following discussion at Public Services Committee, and
requested a two week postponement to keep the timing for the affiliated final plat and r e-zoning
actions together and allow for an additional matter to be worked out.  Reporting for Public Services
Committee, Mr. Cook moved to appr ove on third and f inal reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr.
Cook, seconded by Mr. Shanor, moved to amend by replacing the current language in Section 5 of
the ordinance with the following language:  “That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect
upon its approval and publication and upon the official filing of the affiliated The Bluffs, 10  Filing,th
final p lat m ap w ith t he C ounty C lerk a nd E x-Officio Register o f D eeds f or L aramie C ounty,
Wyoming.”.  Upon inqu iry regarding pur pose of the a mendment, Mr. Ha fner explained the
amendment wording was requested at Committee to keep the annexation, zone change and final plat
all together relative to effective date of the annexation.  He explained there was a concern that if the
plat didn’t get recorded then they would not want the annexation and zone change to be effective.
Mr. Hafner advised Mr. White had offered him an opinion since the Committee meeting that he felt
the statutes require an annexation ordinance be tied to a da te but not tied to filing of a  particular
document to become effective, and requested the amendment be voted down so further discussion
could occur.  Upon inquiry , Dan White, City  Attorney, advised it is not customary  to state an
annexation ordinance becomes effective upon the approval of an affiliated final plat. He noted the
ordinance is usually effective either when it is published or, in addition, on a date specified in the
annexation.  Mr. Whit e stated c ontinuing discussions with Mr. Haf ner will be held to revie w a
potential way to address the applicant’s concern.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. White advised he will
review statutes to determine whether the amendment wording, which is currently contained in the
affiliated re-zoning ordinance, was applicable.  Comment followed regarding an annexation action
being a separate issue from a final plat or zone change since annexation serves as an initial starting
point.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Mr. Shanor
moved to postpone to February 23, 2015, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body.

Bluff and Mountain Road).  K elly Hafner, Benchmark Engineering, agent for the  applicant a nd
property owner, United Western Corporation, requested a two week postponement in order to tie the
zone change with the approval time frame of the annexation.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third
and final reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone to February 23, 2015,
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

Appointments of Brent Groesbeck to the Contractor Licensing Board, and Alane M. West
to the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority Board (DDA).  Mr. Luna moved to approve,
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with
the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointees).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, February 23, 2015 at 6:00
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for renewal applications
for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, and
microbrewery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.
Announcement was made  of t he Re-Appointment of J ames W. Hea rne to the City  of
Cheyenne, Wyoming - Laramie County, State of Wyoming Joint Powers Board for Frontier Park
Improvements, to be confirmed February 23, 2015.
Announcement was made that due to the Presidents’ Day Holiday on Monday, February 16,
2015, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the Municipal
Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee
will meet Tuesday , February 17, 2015 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Service s Committee will meet
Wednesday, February 18, 2015 at Noon.
Under other business:
Upon inquiry regarding a recent financial report involving the separation of the Planning and
Development offices, Mayor Kaysen advised a meeting with the pr ivate sector and stakeholders
would be scheduled in the near future, noting budget preparation for the next fiscal year is affiliated
with the separation.
Upon inquiry regarding the court case status of the Employee Investment Study, Dan White,
City Attorney, briefly reviewed the case filed against the City by the Wyoming Tribune-Eagle; time
lines involving court filings, briefings and oral arguments, and advised an opinion from the District
Court judge has not yet been received.
Dan White, City Attorney, noted the Public Meetings Act allows the governing body to meet
in executive session for considering or tendering offers regarding salaries, wages or other terms and
conditions of employ ment during  neg otiations with the fire  fig hters union.  He r equested the
governing body move into executive session to receive an explanation and summary from himself
and Lois Huff, City Treasurer, regarding proposals to be submitted to the union at the meeting set
for February 11, 2015 when the initial negotiation session begins.  Mr. Shanor moved to meet in
executive session, seconded by Mr. Cook.  M otion carried.  V oting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body.  Mayor Kaysen announced members would meet in executive session in Council
Chambers, not open to the public; re quested the C ity Attorney, City Clerk, and other  staff a s
requested by the City Attorney, be present at the executive session, and stated there would be no
action taken by the governing body as a result of the executive session, and that the regular City
Council meeting would offici ally conclude at the close  of the e xecutive session.  (Clerk’s Note:
Following a brief recess, executive session convened at 6:56 p.m.)

The meeting was officially adjourned at 7:54 p.m. following conclusion of the e xecutive
session.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 February 19, 2015

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2015-02-23 | CLIP_ID: 472 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 472

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
February 23, 2015
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor
[arriving at 6:04 p.m.], Mr Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed  from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.) Upon request by Ms. Williams to remove agenda item #20 from
the Consent Agenda and support being received by Dr. Rinne but no other City Council member, the
item remained on Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 9, 2015.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Special Meetings of the Governing Body on February 11, 2015 and February
14, 2015.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Tyrrell-
Doyle Auto Center) and v acated Council Chambers.  Mr. V aldez moved to approve  payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Brown.  [City Clerk note:  Mr. Shanor joined the meeting]
Upon inquiry , Todd Fe ezer, Assistant Dire ctor, Parks a nd Recre ation Depa rtment, prov ided
information on the following vouchers: Colorado Doorways, Inc. and Construction Specialists, Inc.
(replacement and installation of door  (fire r esistant) at the Children’ s Villag e); Da nt Clay ton
Corporation (additional bleachers for Power’s Field); DM Mailing Service (bandanas for dogs/Dog
Obedience class training (expense covered by registration fees), and Ermold Park and Recreation
Products (ADA compliant p layground surface (engineered wood f ibers) at Neighborhood Park).
Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen advised a voucher payable to B uilders Choice was for services in
conjunction with grant monies the City receives to assist low income homeowners.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception
of Ms. Williams voting “no”.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to Council
Chambers.
Public Hearing for  renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant,
limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, and microbrewery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s
Office.  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, noted the following requests are included with the renewal of
the licenses: The Suite (bar a nd grill license) is requesting an additional dispensing r oom, and
Lambousis Revocable Trust is requesting to park its retail license for one additional year as allowed
by state statute.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been
met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the applications to Finance Committee.

State of Wyoming Joint Powers Board for Frontier Park Improvements.  M r. Shanor moved to
approve, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointee).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 9, 2015, at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions
required by Wyoming Statute §15-1- 402 exist for a pa rcel of land of a pproximately 78.9 acres
situated in the North 286.5 feet of Tract 36; NW¼SE¼SW¼ of Section 4; and a portion of Sections
4 and 5, T.13N., R66W., 6  P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located North of E. Fox Farm Road,th
between Morrie Avenue and Avenue C-4). 
Under other business, Bart Stolp referenced comments he had made during an October 2014
City Council meeting concerning traffic, speed limits and his request for safety improvements on
Van Buren Avenue, near Dildine Elementary School.  He noted, pursuant to discussions with the
City En gineer’s office a nd the  sc hool d istrict, p lans a re in p rogress t o im plement s peed li mit
reductions in the school zone but he ha d been advised that the re st of Van B uren (outside of the
school zone) would remain a 30 m.p.h. area as it is d esignated as a collector street.  M r. Stolp
discussed potential options to reduce the street’s speed limit, outside of the school zone area, to 25
m.p.h.  Upon inquiry , Nathan B eauheim, Acting City  Eng ineer, explained st ate st atutory
requirements that must be followed to change the speed limit from 30 to 25 m.p.h.; ad vised what
area constitutes the school zone and the  City’s signage policy in school zones/crosswalks, and
provided information on the Neighborhood Traffic Management Program (NTMP) adopted, as
updated, by resolution earlier in the meeting.  Comments followed on the City and school district
committee that meets to review traffic issues near schools; composition of committee members; date
of the ne xt committee meet ing, and procedural elements of the r evised NTMP that nee d to be
coordinated and possibly adjusted due to different neighborhood specifics and program requests.
There being  no further  business to come before the g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 7:07 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 March 5, 2015

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2015-03-09 | CLIP_ID: 475 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 475

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
March 9, 2015
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr Valdez and
Ms. Williams.  Absent: Mr. Brown.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed  from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Shanor and Mr. Cook, ag enda item #17 wa s removed from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr. Luna
moved to approve  remaining Consent Agen da items, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carr ied.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 23, 2015.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Special Meetings of the Governing Body on February 20, 24, 25 and 26, 2015.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Tyrrell-
Doyle Auto Center) and v acated Council Chambers.  Mr. V aldez moved to approve  payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a pu blic hearing to de termine whether compliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land of approximately 78.9
acres situated in the North 286.5 feet of Tract 36; NW¼SE¼SW¼ of Section 4; and a portion of
Sections 4 and 5, T.13N., R66W., 6  P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located North of E. Foxth
Farm Road, between Morrie Avenue and Avenue C-4).  Josh Tetzlaff, Planning and Development
office, revie wed the  property to be anne xed and sta ted that re quirements and c ompliance of
annexation conditions contained in state statutes ha d been me t.  He noted that a n aff iliated
preliminary plat has bee n submitt ed and a  final plat and zoning  amendment will also re quire
consideration for approval.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Tetzlaff advised there is one adjacent property that
will remain in the County since the property owner does not wish to seek annexation at this time;
reviewed street improvements within the area that will meet city standards; advised soil testing has
been conducted and that any refuse or contamination will have to be remedied before development
occurs; noted that quite a fe w ea sements run throug h the prope rty and will be shown in the
preliminary plat, and that the applicant has proposed a trail along the north side of the property with
those details being worked out through the platting process.  Comments followed on past use of the
property; topography of the area, and topics that will be determined through the platting process.
There being no further  comments or input and purpose  of t he hearing having been me t, Mayor
Kaysen closed the public hearing.

 RESOLUTION - Re- appropriating the Fiscal Year 2015 budget of the City  of Cheyenne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr.
Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, provided
information re garding the proposed r e-appropriation of $2,000,000 f or pavement mana gement
system improvements relative to the City’s general fund reserve balance status; amount of reserve
funds that are assigned and unassigned; verified that no 1% sales tax monies are involved in the re-
appropriation for the street improvements, and noted use of 1% monies that are dedicated to road
projects.  Discussion included the following topics: General fund reserve status; use of 1% sales tax
monies f or str eet im provements a nd repairs and a mounts c ollected f rom th e 20 11-2014 te rm;
location of streets proposed to be improved; pavement management program; potholes, and on-going
need for funding street maintenance improvements.  In response to inquiries, Nathan Beauheim,
Acting City Engineer, provided information on the pavement management program and road analysis
that was conducted which identified roads requiring capital maintenance, stating the purpose of the
program is to get the most life as possible out of streets; street locations and types of improvements
that would be achieved with the funding; current street improvement projects being accomplished
through use of 1% sales tax funds; annual budget amount dedicated to street repairs and
improvements, and the on-going problem of deteriorating streets due to age and use.  Upon inquiry,
Craig LaVoy, Public Works Department, reviewed criteria the pavement management system utilizes
to d etermine ide ntification o f s treets r equiring ma intenance; a dvised it  is key to g et st reets i n
maintenance vs. dete rioration mode  due to more costs involved once a stre et deteriorates, and
explained the typical life span of streets if properly maintained and conditions that affect street life.
In response to inquiries, Mayor Kaysen and Ms. Huff provided information on 5  penny sales taxth
collection amounts for the 2011-2014 ballot term; estimated amount of overage; percentage of 5th
penny money allocated for street projects, and curr ent 1% proje cts.  Ms. Huff state d if the re -
appropriation is approved, the City would be at a 60 day unassigned reserve level and Mayor Kaysen
indicated a pproximately $5,000,000 is neede d annually  to keep up with stree t mainte nance.
Discussion followed on the proposed re-appropriation of $217,000 for Aquatic Center costs to equal
the Fiscal Year 2014 budget.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation
Director, explained the curr ent budget amount was reduced during the Fisc al Year 2015 budg et
process as costs were unknown at that time with regard to the pool renovation project’s construction
time frames and a ffiliated staffing and oper ational costs.  Mr. Parish provide d information on
expenses incurred due to relocating municipal pool services to various local schools with swimming
pools during the renovation; additional life guards required for the remote locations and janitorial
and affiliated costs assessed for use of the sites.  He reviewed the costs affiliated with the pool
renovation 6  penny sales tax project; grant received, and use of Menard’s land sale proceeds forth
certain department projects, and verified the funding from reserves will not go toward the renovation
project construction costs.  F ollowing further discussion, Mr. Shanor moved to amend the  re-
appropriation amount by subtracting $600,000 to reflect a reduction of $100,000 from Parks and
Recreation’s request and a reduction of $500,000 from Miscellaneous Items (streets), seconded by
Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Par ish spoke in oppositi on to the amendment, e xplaining the newly renovated
portion of the Aquatics Center is scheduled to open Ma y 1  and, in addition to past expenses hest
previously noted, will involve additional costs to operate  through the end of the fiscal year.  Jeff
Dierksen spoke in opposition to removing the funding for street maintenance, commenting that it
appears the maintenance program is already underfunded.  Discussion included the following topics:
Concern with reducing the general fund reserve level; funding for economic development; amount
of 6  penny sales tax monies needed for the Aquatics Center renovation project, and funding useth
requirements utilizing Menard’s land sale proceeds for Parks and Recreation Department projects,
including Converse girls softball field and Powers baseball field improvements.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
Parish and Mayor Kaysen provided information on the Aquatics Center renovation construction costs
and benefits of the ball field improvements respectively.  During discussion, Dr. Rinne raised a point
of order that discussion was not g ermane to the motion on the floor with May or Kaysen stating
comments should pertain to the motion to amend.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr.
Johnson, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Cook, Mr. Roybal,
Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body present. (#5660)

Agenda) carried.
Announcement was made of the Appointment of Anthony  Durst to the International Fire
Code Board of Appeals, to be confirmed March 23, 2015. 
Under other business:
Jeff Dierksen referenced a petition that had been previously submitted regarding traffic and
safety concerns involving a portion of Pinto L ane and expressed his belief tha t mitigation of the
concerns have  not been c ompletely addre ssed throug h the Neig hborhood Tr affic Mana gement
Program (NTMP) process.  He commented on the resolution amending the NTMP the governing
body recently adopted, requested the r esolution be rec onsidered and stated his rea sons for the
request.  Discussion followed on funding for the NTMP program; identification of and accessibility
to information of ava ilable grants, applicant eligibility, and the  grant process affiliated with the
Metropolitan Pl anning Or ganization ( MPO).  U pon in quiry, N athan B eauheim, Ac ting City
Engineer, provided information on traffic signs that will be installed and referenced the final report
issued by the MPO director.  Mayor Kaysen advised he would request the MPO director provide
information pursuant to the discussion on g rants to governing body members as well as the f inal
report on the Pinto Lane corridor study.
In response to inquiry, Mayor Kaysen provided an update on the status of the division of the
Planning and Development offices is to separate departments and indicated a list of stakeholders
proposed to be involved in a n up coming meeting  to discuss the separ ation of off ices will be
forthcoming, noting the topic is c urrently being evaluated as part of the Fiscal Year 2016 budget
preparation process.
There b eing no further busine ss to come bef ore the  g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 7:45 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
April 13, 2015
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook (arriving at 6:02 p.m.), Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Roybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  Mayor Kaysen recognized members of th e Mighty Eagles 4-H Club
present at the meeting who then led recitation of the pledge of allegiance.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed  from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Luna moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by
Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 23, 2015.  Moti on (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Roybal each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Amer i-Tech HVAC Service s and Ty rrell-Doyle Chevrolet r espectively) and va cated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Valdez .  Upon inquiry, Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director; Kari Kivisto, Leisure Services
Director and Mayor Kaysen provided information available on vouchers payable to Environmental
Health (fee collected for reviewing plats); refunds to several individuals for deposits for “Learn to
Play” hockey equipment rentals, and The Nature Conservancy (cost of easement share for property
located next to the Belvoir Ranch per recent approval by resolution).  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the
vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Roybal returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comment regarding the intent of the
City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding to the Wyoming Business Council’s Business
Ready Community, Community Enhancement Grant Program, to assist in funding the West Edge
Streetscape and Civic Center Commons.  C indy Schneider, Grants Manager, advised the g rant
request amount is $500,000.00 with a 50% City match.  She noted elements of the project for which
the funding  will be used and r eviewed the  g rant a pproval proc ess and time fr ames.  Ge ne
MacDonald,  Ayres Associates, project consultant, provided information on the project and advised
the grant will assist with streetscape design and implementation throughout the West Edge District
and Civic Center Commons area.  Upon inquiry, Mr. MacDonald reviewed the geographic area of
the Civic Center Commons portion of the project.  Frank Cole expressed concern with the proposed
removal of the par king lot west of the municipal building used by  the public during  numerous
activities held at the Civic Center .  Mayor Kaysen advised as part of the grant process involving
planning and stree tscape design, the parking issues w ill be addre ssed.  Ther e being no further
comments and  purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
ORDINANCE - Repealing Chapter 5.76, Temporary or Transient Merchants, in its entirety
and re-cr eating Chapter 5.76, Te mporary or Tra nsient Merc hants; Residential Door-To- Door
Solicitors, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Reg ulations, of the code  of the City  of Chey enne,
Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final
reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. S hanor moved to amend the de finition of “Solicit and
“solicitation” in Section 5.76.020, Definitions, by striking the words “conducted by an individual
approved as a r esidential door-to-door solicitor” so the se ntence will instead read “Solicit” and
“solicitation” mean and include any one or more of the following activities . . .”, with subsections
numbered 1, 2 and 3 following the changed wording, seconded by Ms. Williams.  It was advised the
amendment be tter d efines the activity  of solicitation.  Comm ent followed in support of the
amendment and ordinance generally.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook  voting “no”.  Ma in motion as amended c arried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#4064)
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the North 286.5 feet of Tract

Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of E. Fox Farm Road, betwe en Morrie Avenue and

Utilities, and Morgan Martin to the Historic Preservation Board, to be confirmed April 27, 2015.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, April 27, 2015 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the City of Cheyenne
Comprehensive Consolidated Plan 2015 - 2019 which incorporates the Annual Action Plan 2015-
2016, City of Cheyenne Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) entitlement.  
Under other business, Ms. Williams questioned the status of the graffiti clean-up program
with Mayor Kaysen noting there are no further funds, or supplies which were previously provided
by the community, for the program available through the police department.  
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body , the meeting w as
officially adjourned at 6:32 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 April 23, 2015

April 27, 2015
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor, Mr Valdez
and Ms. Williams.  Absent: Mr. Cook.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Dan White,
City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Shanor recused himself from participation in voting due to any
appearance of influence on Consent Agenda items involving the State Loan and Investment Board,
and vacated Council Chambers.  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Luna and Ms. Williams, agenda item #17 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request
by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Brown and Mr. Roybal, agenda item #21(c)
was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda
items, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Council
Chambers.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on April 13, 2015.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Roybal each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Amer i-Tech HVAC Service s and Ty rrell-Doyle Chevrolet r espectively) and va cated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Luna.  Inquiry for infor mation was m ade for voucher s payable to Cheyenne Light Fuel and
Power (status of last energy audits and review); Hageman Earth Cycle (commercial grade topsoil for
new tee (golf) boxes); Lyle Signs Inc. (traffic signs), and Triple J Pumping (porta potty services at
the Compost Facility).  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director; Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation
Director; N athan B eauheim, A cting Ci ty E ngineer, a nd M ayor K aysen p rovided in formation
pertaining to the inquiry.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the g overning body
present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Roybal
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input regarding the City of
Cheyenne Comprehensive Consolidated Plan 2015 - 2019 which inc orporates the Annual Action
Plan 2015-2016, City of Cheyenne Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) entitlement.
Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development Office, provided a brief overview of the
history and purpose of the CDBG program, noting the City has received entitlement grant funds since
1975.  She summarized prog ram objectives and the Advisory  Board’s recommendation for 11
eligible projects to be funded through the entitlement allocation funding amount of $404,588.00, as
well as program administration and recaptured funding.  Ms Widauf explained the grant application
process, pu blic no tifications a nd op portunities f or inp ut, a nd ro le of  the Advisory Cou ncil i n
reviewing and recommending projects to be funded.  Upon inquiry regarding information contained
in the Plan on barriers to affordable housing relating to public policies that include land development
costs, impact fees and growth management and design regulations, Ms. Widhauf advised they are
very restricted in what CDB G funds ca n be alloca ted toward but they  will take the topic under
consideration.  There being no further comments and  purpose of the public hearing having been met,
Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.

governing body present.

Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.

Program, to assist in funding the West Edge Streetscape and Civic Center Commons.  Motion (per

[CA] RESOLUTION - Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2015 budget of the City of Cheyenne

(#5666)

- 2014 Countywide Consensus Block Grant funding for a Curb, Gutter and Concrete Rehab Project,
2015 - 2 016 Countywide Consensus Block Grant for the Larson Road Reconstruction Project.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5668)
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for
Swan Ranch Rail Park, 10  Filing, situated on portions of the NE¼N E¼ of Section 28 and theth
NW¼NW¼ of Section 27, T.13N., R67W., 6  P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located west ofth
and adjacent to Berwick Road, north of Tundra Drive).  James Perrott commented in opposition to
the resolution, noting concerns involve his family’s ancestry.  Troy Thompson, Laramie County
Commissioner, a dvised th e Cou nty Com missioners had passed t he re solution wi thout a
recommended City staff condition, and expressed concern with Wyoming case law relative to the
City’s ability to place a condition on a County plat within one mile of the City’s boundaries. Ms.
Williams moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Discussion followed on previously approved
County plats that had included the same proposed City staff condition that had been approved by the
County Commissioners; final plat resolution approval process and the effect on the developer and
proposed plat if the City and County did not adopt identical provisions; desire to resolve the issue
of County plats within the one mile boundary with the County Commissioners in the near future;
possible postponement of the item, and f uture annexation of the prope rty.  Upon inquiry , Brad
Emmons, AVI, agent for the applicant, clarified the site is curr ently under construction and that
postponement of the item would not affect construction; advised purpose of the plat is to cure a legal
subdivision deficiency, and stated the plat property involves one lot and any future vacation that
might be contemplated would probably not be feasible.  Mr. Brown moved to amend to include the
following City staff condition on the plat (map), seconded by Mr. Johnson:  “The undersigned do
hereby acknowledge that this final plat is submitted to the governing body of the City of Cheyenne
for approval pursuant to W.S. §34-12-103 and the undersigned owner(s) and their legal successors
and/or assigns forever do(es) hereby covenant and agree that, following the approval of this plat by
the said Governing Body, no alteration, amendment, or vacation of this plat, in whole or in part, shall
be valid unless such alteration, amendment, or vacation is approved by the said Governing Body.”.
Commissioner Thompson requested the word “vacation” be removed from the proposed condition
wording, and expressed the County and City need to work together on this issue, perhaps through
a state legislation change.  Mr. Perrott commented on rights to the property.  Mr. Brown raised a
point of order that Mr. Perrott’s comments were not germane to the motion to amend on the floor
with Mayor Kaysen calling for further discussion from City Council members.  Discussion followed
on the proposed amendment; annexation timing ; r emoving the word “vacation” from the s taff
condition; whether the amendment was needed at this time, and process and effect if the City and
County adopted conflicting requirements.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Mr. Emmons provided
information on the proje ct and f uture annexation; expressed his belief tha t there c an not be two
separate (conflicting) plat maps, and reiterated his previous statement that the purpose of the plat is
to correct a legal subdivision deficiency.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Mr.
Johnson and Mayor Kaysen. Voting “no” - - Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roy bal, Mr. Shanor, Mr .
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present.  (#5669)
[CA]  RESOLUTION - Adopting the City of Cheyenne’s Comprehensive Consolidated Plan 2015-
2019, prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office -- referred to Finance
Committee (sponsors -- Mr. Brown and Ms. Williams).

Brown).

[CA] Memorandum of  Under standing between the City  of Chey enne, T ransit Division, and
Laramie County, Wyoming, to provide public transportation services to Laramie County Community
College, City/County Health Department, and an area of Laramie County adjacent to the City.

Right-of-Way Encroachment Agreement between the City  of Chey enne and the State of

James Perrott commented on the City’s jurisdiction involving the Swan Ranch.  Ms.  Williams

Project, advised the agreement was drafted between the attorney general and City attorney to include

campaign w ith the community  and spec ifically those in the ar ea r egarding desig n plans and
construction.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen advised the  island improvements, when c ompleted,
located within the ROW will become the City’s maintenance responsibility, and that the State, as the
developer, has assumed the responsibility to notify the neighborhood of the project.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6317)
[CA] Contract between the City of C heyenne, Police Department, and Absolute Shre dding for
document destruction services -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Contract betwe en the City  of Chey enne, Sanitation Division, and Clean H arbors
Environmental Services, Inc. for hazardous waste disposal services at the Cheyenne Household
Hazardous Waste Facility (Solid Waste Fund) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and the
City of Torrington, Wyoming, Police Department, for law enforcement extraterritorial assistance --
referred to Finance Committee.   
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to City Contract No. 6219 between the City of Cheyenne and
Recco, Inc., dba Five-R Co., to establish the Guaranteed Maximum Price (GMP) for the Cheyenne
Botanic Gardens Conservatory Expansion & Renovation Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use
Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to City  Contract No. 6000 be tween the City of Chey enne,
Engineering Department, and AVI Professional Corporation, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for additional
construction management services for the Logan Avenue Reconstruction Design Project (2011-2014
1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 5511 between the City of Cheyenne, Public

[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 5927 between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and
Recreation Department, and AV I Professional Corpor ation, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for additional
professional services to complete the Polk Avenue Project (Greenway Funds) -- referred to Finance
Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-22-15 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering
Department, and Qua lity Electric, Ch eyenne, Wyoming, for the Cobra  LED Lighting Upgrade
Project (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid in an
amount not to exceed $73,428.00). (#6318)
[CA] Consideration of B id #R-25-15 for furnishing  front end loa d container s for the City ’s
Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Management).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted
bid from Wastequip Manufacturing Company, LLC, Statesville, NC, in an amount not to ex ceed
$66,490.00).
[CA]  Consideration o f B id # M-17-15 fo r a  co ntract b etween t he C ity o f Cheyenne, 1%
Construction Division, and JTL Group, Inc., d/b/a Knife River, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the Fiscal
Year 2015 City Overlay Part 2 Project (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of B id #M-26-15 for  a contr act between the City of Chey enne, Facilities
Maintenance Division, and Amer i-Tech HVAC Service s, I nc. of Che yenne, Wy oming, f or
Mechanical and HVAC System Maintenance for 14 City Buildings -- referred to Finance Committee.
Appointments of Mary Guthrie to the Board of Public Utilities, and Morgan Martin to the
Historic Preservation Board.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who
did not vote (Mayoral appointees).
[CA] Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne at
the June 13, 2015 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Business Meeting.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried (Mayor Richard L. Kaysen, Official Voting Delegate; City Clerk Carol Intlekofer,
Alternate Delegate).  
Under other business:
Major Dorr expressed concerns regarding the elimination of the City parking lot west of the
Municipal Building due to proposed plans for the West Edge/Civic Center Commons Project, and
requested consideration that the parking lot be retained to meet parking needs for those accessing
the Civic Center and Municipal Building.  Mr. Dorr also expressed concerns with the need to fix
street pot holes in the downtown area.
James Perrott commented on Belvoir Ranch property taxes, the City’s possession of the land,
and his family’s genealogy involving the land.  
David Nicely discussed his conce rns regarding the con struction schedule f or the L ogan
Avenue Reconstruction Project and negative effect the project is having on local businesses in the
area. 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 7:21 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
